### q_9e5a60 (dataflip500, select, synthetic_tabular_classification) score=0.00 noKB_score=1.00 gold=B pred=A cited=K1009,K1015,K1056,K1061 retrieved=none
explanation: Choice A best balances the piecewise boundary’s need for nonlinear depth with residual signal propagation and smooth SiLU activations, while sharing the same RMSprop training budget. Choice B is deeper but lacks residual connections and uses ReLU, making optimization harder at this modest rate. Choice C is easiest to optimize but too shallow to model the piecewise boundary effectively; its ReLU activation also gives up the smooth-activation advantage seen for sufficiently trained tabular classification models. <cited>K1009,K1056,K1015,K1061</cited>
comment: Choice B won because this was a low-progress tabular CE regime where the smooth-activation advantage does not apply. The piecewise-linear ReLU network’s greater depth matched the kinked rule better than A’s SiLU residual network, while C was too shallow. I overextended K1056’s smooth-activation preference and residual-optimization intuition; K1009 explicitly scopes shallow/smooth preferences and does not establish a total architecture ranking. K1056’s threshold condition (Delta>=.1) was the key missed qualifier.
hypothesis: For tabular piecewise-boundary classification with RMSprop at lr=1e-4 and roughly 16k samples, when training displacement remains below 0.1, prefer a sufficiently deep plain ReLU MLP over similarly sized residual SiLU or shallow residual alternatives. The mechanism is that ReLU represents the rule’s slope discontinuity directly, while SiLU smoothing is only beneficial after sufficient optimization. This is falsifiable by matched RMSprop grids varying depth, residuals, and activation while measuring Delta and test CE; the predicted reversal is that SiLU overtakes ReLU once Delta reaches approximately 0.1 or higher. I could not access the requested history/lab backtest interface in this run, so no reliable hit/miss count is reported.

The largest-Δ choice wins 85% of optimizer-only and ~85% of mixed questions (after the lr-ceiling exceptions); a 1.5-3× Δ edge usually beats any architecture advantage. Adam/AdamW/RMSprop: Δ≈lr·T. Adagrad (zero-init accumulator, steps decay ~lr/√t): Δ≈2·lr·√T. SGD: Δ≈lr·T·|g|/(1−m) with raw grad |g|≈0.01 (bigram LM 0.003, MSE regression with momentum 0.03 — K1019), i.e. plain SGD ≈ Adam at lr/100, SGD m=0.9 ≈ Adam at lr/10. The largest-Δ choice wins 85% of optimizer-only and ~85% of mixed questions (after the lr-ceiling exceptions); a 1.5-3× Δ edge usually beats any architecture advantage.
